# w-bfclrt: offline runtime custody

Disposition: **blocker stays external/open; exact runtime closure remains incomplete.**

The original w-bfclrt observations below remain historical; the current direct-member frontier is updated by w-bfclmem in the closure section. The packet binds the retained Python source imports and a bounded local candidate runtime. It does not establish a runnable official scorer or select a local-model handler. Only offline imports and static inspection were performed; no differential, inference, GPU use, or official scorer execution was performed (test scope: `tests/fixtures/hg_bfclrt/smoke.py:1`; disposition/counts: `tests/fixtures/hg_bfclrt/summary.jsonl:1`).

## Evidence states and exact search scope

**FOUND** means the cited bytes/metadata/import were read. **ABSENT** always means absent from the enumerated search scope below, not the whole machine. **UNKNOWN** includes unread/opaque artifacts, source branches beyond the recorded frontier, exact build provenance, and compatibility not actually exercised. Installed trees, cached wheels, conda archives, and loaded ELF objects are separate artifacts; a shared package/version is not a byte binding.

Reproduce the bounded search with `export TMPDIR=/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/hg-work/bfclrt/tmp; export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1; python3 -B -u tests/fixtures/hg_bfclrt/collect.py search`. The exact enumerated paths and outcomes follow; symlink aliases of site directories are deduplicated. Non-ZIP pip response bodies were enumerated but not unpacked; their package contents are UNKNOWN. Other users, other wheelhouses, unenumerated environments, unnamed source archives, and symlinked cache subtrees are UNKNOWN. No whole-machine absence is asserted.

## Source graph: exact syntactic edges, guarded branches kept separate

All retained source identities are recomputed in `source-modules.jsonl`; every explicit import statement is in `source-edges.jsonl`, with verbatim statement, original `file:line`, containing function/class and if/try guards. Each line is an edge record, not a guessed runtime execution. Package initializers are retained source members. The packet does not invent dynamic targets or equate a metadata dependency with a Python import. Counts and prior-inventory equality: `tests/fixtures/hg_bfclrt/summary.jsonl:1`.

The checker imports `MODEL_CONFIG_MAPPING` eagerly, whose imports pull every retained provider/local handler into the module import graph. Selecting Simple/Python does not remove those import statements. The runner/helper import the same mapping. The parser utility eagerly imports both language parsers and tenacity. Their module-level `Language(..., name)` and `Parser.set_language` calls require the old Tree-sitter API; the installed 0.24.0 smoke records `Parser_has_set_language=false`.

Full dispatch edges (including each handler) are explicitly enumerated in the source-edge packet; the per-requirement table below cites each external root edge. Multi-turn dynamic imports and expression-evaluation call sites remain branch-specific unresolved behavior, recorded verbatim in `source-edges.jsonl`. No expression was evaluated by this worker.

Third-party recursion is represented in two distinct ways: `metadata-edges.jsonl` gives each Requires-Dist header with original metadata file/line, marker and requested extras; `installed-edges-*.jsonl` gives imports read from actually observed Python files plus the unexecuted transformers/wandb entry files. Each grouped edge has its original source path/line. `module-resolution-index.jsonl` lists the read module-to-path map; `module-frontier-*.jsonl` explicitly lists unread targets and dynamic calls as UNKNOWN. This is a bounded graph with an exact frontier, not a complete transitive runtime claim. From-import symbol names are preserved and are not assumed to be child modules. The traversal/marker rules are executable in `collect.py` and `summarize.py`.

ELF objects are SHA-256 bound in `elf-*.jsonl`, with exact `LC_ALL=C readelf -h -d -p .comment PATH` output and the version-needs excerpt from `readelf -V PATH`. `native-links.jsonl` records each DT_NEEDED edge and same-process mapped SONAME/name candidates. A unique mapped candidate is not proof of an individual relocation binding. `/proc/self/maps` observations appear at the end of each smoke record. Exact compiled-member build Python patch version, full toolchain and reproducible build provenance remain UNKNOWN; wheel tags/ELF architecture/.comment do not prove them.

Conda metadata path membership can be stale after pip upgrades. `native-owners.jsonl` separately records local archive hashes and streaming comparisons of actual archive members against installed native bytes. A false `matches` value is an observed mismatch, not custody. A true value binds those exact bytes; prefix-patched files may differ and require their own documented transformation. These comparisons do not reconstruct an environment.

Resolved import paths/hashes and exact import failures are in `smoke-base.jsonl` and `smoke-tree.jsonl`. The base smoke imports NumPy/Pandas and available SDK utilities; it does not run `eval_runner`, construct a model handler, parse fabricated calls, or execute a checker. The newer Tree-sitter alternative imports successfully but both BFCL parser imports fail for missing grammar modules. The old API mismatch is independently visible without constructing a language.

## Licenses/notices bound to bytes actually read

`license-index.jsonl` inventories each retained license/notice text, its source record/archive member, exact hash and conservative text identifier. `licenses.jsonl`, `archive-licenses.jsonl` and `native-licenses.jsonl` distinguish installed, wheel and conda/system sources. Copies preserve verbatim text; an empty license directory entry establishes no license. Licenses of wrong-version packages do not satisfy missing pins. Conda licenses for stale mismatching package members do not license replacement bytes.

- Apache-2.0 texts require retaining license/attribution notices, marking modified files and reproducing applicable NOTICE contents; actual Boto3/Botocore/S3transfer notices are separate retained files. Patent terms remain in the full text.
- MIT/BSD-style texts require preserving copyright, permission/conditions and disclaimers; BSD no-endorsement clauses apply where present. Tqdm has its own dual-license and derived-work conditions, not a single blanket MIT assumption.
- NumPy wheel license text also identifies bundled OpenBLAS/LAPACK/GCC runtime and other licenses; the installed MKL variant has separate Intel terms. Redistribution of a chosen variant must carry that variant’s licenses/notices. System and conda licenses are not interchangeable.
- MPL/LGPL/GPL/PSF/custom or dual-license material requires the exact applicable text, source/relinking/exception conditions and component scope, rather than replacing it with the top-level distribution identifier. This packet inventories texts; it does not certify redistribution compliance.

For the basis of those obligations and mixed terms, see the per-member verbatim files above and `license-index.jsonl`; native/license source bindings appear in `native-owners.jsonl`. Licenses/notices of missing pinned Tree-sitter/grammars/SDKs and unresolved transitive or dynamic members are UNKNOWN. Full license scope of some bundled native objects remains UNKNOWN even where a top-level LICENSE was found. System notice lookup scope is exactly `native-license-search.jsonl`.

SBOMs contain 550 component declarations, not 550 measured build inputs; full component licence texts remain UNKNOWN except separately cited notices (`tests/fixtures/hg_bfclattest/sbom-components.jsonl:1` through `:550`).

`TMPDIR=/tmp/bfclattest-tmp python3 -B tests/fixtures/hg_bfclattest/collect.py system` records `/sbin/ldconfig -p`, selects exact SONAME rows whose ABI tuple starts `(libc6,x86-64`, then reads exact files, SONAME/version tables, dpkg ownership/version and copyright paths. Provider evidence is static availability; RPATH/RUNPATH, loader namespaces, preload, symbol interposition and unexercised dlopen prevent a claim about actual loader selection. System ELF bytes/readelf outputs are preserved under `/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/hg-work/bfclattest/system/`; hashes and exact commands are in `system.jsonl`.

`TMPDIR=/tmp/bfclattest-tmp python3 -B tests/fixtures/hg_bfclattest/signatures.py` verifies all 43 DSSE payload signatures with their supplied leaf public keys.
